Now let me provide you with a comprehensive exploration report:

## Comprehensive Project Exploration Report

I've thoroughly explored the DuckDB Data Agent project. Here's what I found:

### 1. **Overall Project Structure**

The project is a full-stack AI data analysis platform with these key directories:

```
duckdb-data-agent/
├── backend/              # FastAPI Python backend (agent logic)
├── frontend/             # React + TypeScript UI
├── sidecar/              # TypeScript container (Claude Agent SDK)
├── e2e/                  # Playwright E2E tests
├── utils/                # Utilities
├── docs/                 # Documentation
└── docker-compose.yml    # Multi-container orchestration
```

**Key Files:**
- `/backend/app/agent.py` - Core orchestrator (535 lines, critical)
- `/sidecar/src/server.ts` - Sidecar Express server (361 lines)
- `/backend/app/proxy.py` - API credential proxy (135 lines)
- `/backend/app/container_manager.py` - Docker container lifecycle (310 lines)
- `/backend/app/config.py` - Environment variable configuration
- `/backend/app/mcp_sse.py` - MCP tool definitions & SSE transport

---

### 2. **Claude Agent SDK Usage**

The project uses the **Claude Agent SDK** in a sophisticated **containerized mode**:

**Architecture Pattern:**
```
Browser
  ↓
FastAPI Backend (Python)
  ├→ Creates short-lived UUID token (proxy_token_store)
  ├→ Spins up Docker container (sidecar)
  └→ Proxies API calls (credential swapping)
       ↓
Sidecar Container (Node.js)
  └→ TypeScript server (Express)
      └→ Calls SDK's query() function
          ├→ System prompt with orchestrator instructions
          ├→ Subagent definitions (sql-analyst, chart-builder)
          └→ MCP server configuration (SSE transport)
               ↓
         Returns stream of messages to backend
```

**Key Code Pattern (agent.py lines 164-535):**
- `stream_chat()` async generator function
- Creates `proxy_token_store.create_token()` UUID before spinning container
- Passes environment variables including token to container
- Streams SSE events from sidecar back to client
- Extracts chart specs from subagent assistant messages
- Revokes token after session ends (`finally` block)

**SDK Message Types Handled:**
- `stream_event` - Token-level streaming (thinking, text deltas)
- `assistant` - Complete assistant messages with tool_use blocks
- `user` - Tool results with `tool_use_result.content` (actual subagent output)
- `result` - Final session result
- `system` - Session initialization

---

### 3. **LLM API Calls & Credential Management**

**Credential Flow:**

```python
# backend/app/config.py
ANTHROPIC_API_KEY = os.getenv("ANTHROPIC_API_KEY", "")  # Real key from .env
PROXY_BASE_URL = os.getenv("PROXY_BASE_URL", "http://127.0.0.1:8000")
ANTHROPIC_MODEL = os.getenv("ANTHROPIC_MODEL", "claude-sonnet-4-6")
SQL_SUBAGENT_MODEL = os.getenv("SQL_SUBAGENT_MODEL", "haiku")
CHART_SUBAGENT_MODEL = os.getenv("CHART_SUBAGENT_MODEL", "haiku")
```

**Proxy Mechanism (proxy.py lines 26-61):**
- `ProxyTokenStore` class manages session-scoped UUIDs
- Tokens have 10-minute TTL by default
- Each agent session gets a unique UUID token
- Proxy at `/anthropic/{path}` validates and swaps tokens
- Real API key never leaves FastAPI backend

**Container Environment Variables (agent.py lines 189-196):**
```python
env: dict[str, str] = {
    "ANTHROPIC_API_KEY": session_token,  # UUID, not real key
    "ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL": f"{PROXY_BASE_URL}/anthropic",
}
if LANGFUSE_ENABLED:
    env["LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY"] = LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY
    env["LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY"] = LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY
    env["LANGFUSE_BASE_URL"] = LANGFUSE_BASE_URL
```

**Proxy Validation (proxy.py lines 73-92):**
- Validates session token against store
- Returns 401 if invalid or expired
- Swaps UUID for real `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` before upstream call
- Reads-only endpoints (e.g., `/v1/models`) pass through without token

---

### 4. **Two Modes: Agent vs Container**

The project supports two execution modes:

**A. Container Mode (Default - Production & Development)**
- Used when `container_manager` is initialized
- Spins up per-session gVisor-sandboxed Docker containers
- Each container runs Node.js + Claude Code CLI + sidecar server
- Located in `/sidecar/` directory
- **Advantages:** Security isolation, multi-tenant safety, sandbox execution

**B. Agent Mode (Fallback)**
- Would run Claude Agent SDK directly in Python process
- Not fully implemented in this codebase
- Code path exists but `stream_chat()` throws if `container_manager` is None
- **Advantages:** Faster startup, simpler debugging

**Current Implementation:**
- `container_manager.py` initializes Docker client
- `ContainerManager.create()` spawns container with:
  - Read-only rootfs
  - All capabilities dropped (`cap_drop=["ALL"]`)
  - Memory limit (512m default)
  - CPU limit (0.5 cores default)
  - Tmpfs mounts for `/tmp` and `/home` (50m each)
  - gVisor runtime (`runsc`) or standard runc

---

### 5. **Docker & Docker Compose Configuration**

**docker-compose.yml Structure:**

```yaml
services:
  app:  # FastAPI backend
    build: ./backend/Dockerfile
    image: duckdb-data-agent:latest
    ports: ["${APP_PORT:-10000}:10000"]
    env_file: backend/.env
    volumes: ["/var/run/docker.sock:/var/run/docker.sock"]
    networks: [agent-sandbox]
    
  sidecar:  # Built but not started (spawned on-demand)
    build: ./sidecar/Dockerfile
    image: duckdb-agent-sidecar:latest
    profiles: [sidecar]  # Only builds with --profile sidecar

networks:
  agent-sandbox:
    external: true  # Must be created by setup-network.sh
```

**Backend Dockerfile (Two-stage build):**
1. Stage 1: Builds React frontend with Node 20
2. Stage 2: Python 3.12 slim, copies built frontend

**Sidecar Dockerfile (Two-stage build):**
1. Stage 1: Compiles TypeScript with tsc
2. Stage 2: Node 20 + globally installed Claude CLI

**Key Environment Setup:**
- Backend reads from `backend/.env` file
- Sidecar receives environment via request payload
- `PROXY_BASE_URL` differs between modes:
  - Dev: `http://host.docker.internal:8000`
  - Docker Compose: `http://duckdb-data-agent:10000`

---

### 6. **Existing Proxy & Gateway Patterns**

**Current Proxy Pattern (proxy.py):**
- **Type:** Anthropic API reverse proxy
- **Location:** `/anthropic/{path}` route
- **Function:** Token validation + credential swapping
- **Scope:** Session-scoped short-lived UUIDs

**Proxy Request Flow:**
```
Client (Sidecar)
  ↓ x-api-key: <UUID>
Proxy validates token → swaps for real key
  ↓ x-api-key: sk-ant-...
Upstream: api.anthropic.com
```

**Proxy Features:**
- Multi-use tokens per session (not single-use)
- TTL enforcement with background cleanup
- Explicit revocation when stream ends
- Skips certain headers (no host, transfer-encoding, etc.)
- Preserves anthropic-beta header for extended features

**MCP SSE Server (mcp_sse.py):**
- Starlette-based MCP server
- Tools: `execute_sql`, `ask_user_question`, `render_chart`
- Session-specific DuckDB instances
- Supports user interaction pauses

---

### 7. **Environment Variables & Secrets Management**

**Configuration Hierarchy (config.py):**
```python
ANTHROPIC_API_KEY          # Required, from .env
PROXY_BASE_URL             # Default: http://127.0.0.1:8000
ANTHROPIC_MODEL            # Default: claude-sonnet-4-6
SQL_SUBAGENT_MODEL         # Default: haiku
CHART_SUBAGENT_MODEL       # Default: haiku
LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY        # Optional
LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY        # Optional
LANGFUSE_BASE_URL          # Default: https://cloud.langfuse.com
MAX_TOTAL_SIZE_BYTES       # Default: 500MB
CONTAINER_IMAGE            # Default: duckdb-agent-sidecar:latest
CONTAINER_RUNTIME          # Default: runc (or runsc for gVisor)
CONTAINER_MEMORY_LIMIT     # Default: 512m
CONTAINER_CPU_LIMIT        # Default: 0.5
CONTAINER_MAX_LIFETIME_SECONDS # Default: 600
CONTAINER_NETWORK          # Default: agent-sandbox
CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS       # Default: localhost:5173,localhost:8000
```

**Secrets Handling:**
- `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` loaded from `.env` file only (never in code)
- Langfuse credentials loaded from `.env`
- Session tokens are UUIDs, never the real key
- Sidecar container receives token in environment, never real key
- Token store in memory with TTL (600 seconds)
- Background cleanup task every 60 seconds

**File-based Configuration:**
- Uses `python-dotenv` to load `.env` file
- `.env.example` provides template
- Backend `.env` is git-ignored

---

### 8. **Recent Development Direction**

**Latest 5 Commits (as of Feb 26):**

1. **0a54b17c** - "feat: interleave text and charts in answer block for better readability" (#32)
   - Frontend improvement for answer rendering

2. **4069f894** - "fix: clean up orphaned sidecar containers on shutdown and startup" (#31)
   - `container_manager.shutdown_all()` cleans labeled containers
   - Startup cleanup via `_cleanup_by_label()` in lifespan

3. **20520f33** - "update" (#30)
   - Metadata/docs update

4. **91e63df5** - "feat(e2e): add Playwright E2E test framework with YAML scenarios" (#29)
   - Testing infrastructure added

5. **a27bfe59** - "Merge pull request #28 from cyyeh/feat/animated-chart-support"
   - Animated Plotly charts with frames support

**Development Trends:**
- Focus on container isolation and stability (orphaned container cleanup)
- UX improvements (chart interleaving)
- Testing infrastructure (E2E Playwright)
- Enhanced visualization support (animated charts)
- Recent branch focus: `feature/llm-proxy` suggests API gateway work

---

### 9. **Key Integration Points**

**Request Flow:**
```
Browser (/api/chat)
  ↓
ChatRequest with message, session_id, conversation_history
  ↓
stream_chat() in agent.py
  ↓
ContainerManager.create(session_id, env)
  ↓
Docker SDK spins container with image duckdb-agent-sidecar:latest
  ↓
Container runs: node dist/server.js
  ↓
POST /query with:
  - message, system_prompt, model
  - agents: {sql-analyst, chart-builder definitions}
  - mcp_server_url: http://proxy:10000/mcp/sse?session_id=X
  - env: {ANTHROPIC_API_KEY: UUID, ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL: .../anthropic}
  ↓
Sidecar SDK.query() → calls Claude with subagents
  ↓
MCP requests → /anthropic/{path} proxy validation
  ↓
SSE stream back to backend
  ↓
Backend filters subagent messages, extracts charts
  ↓
Client receives SSE with events: thinking, answer, tool_call, subagent_end
```

---

### 10. **Architecture Highlights**

**Strengths:**
- Excellent credential isolation (UUID token layer)
- Per-session container isolation with gVisor
- Clean separation of concerns (orchestrator, subagents, tools)
- Langfuse tracing integration for observability
- Stateful session management with DuckDB persistence
- Intelligent chart extraction from SDK messages

**Key Design Patterns:**
1. **Token Proxy Pattern** - UUID intermediate for credential protection
2. **Message Stream Reconstruction** - Capturing chart specs from tool_use inputs
3. **Subagent Text Accumulation** - Handling SDK's final message omission
4. **Container Per-Session** - Isolation with lifecycle management
5. **MCP Tool Binding** - Session-scoped database instances

**Security Model:**
- Defense-in-depth with token layer + container isolation
- No host filesystem access from containers
- Read-only rootfs
- All capabilities dropped
- Background cleanup of orphaned containers

---

This exploration reveals a sophisticated, production-ready architecture that balances security, scalability, and user experience. The proxy infrastructure is well-designed for credential management, and the container isolation provides strong multi-tenant boundaries.

Based on the search results, here's a comprehensive overview of TensorZero's LLM gateway proxy architecture and features for 2025-2026:

## Overview

TensorZero builds open-source tools for production-grade LLM applications: LLM gateway, observability, optimization, evaluations, and experimentation. TensorZero is an open-source stack for industrial-grade LLM applications with a Gateway designed to access every LLM provider through a unified API, built for performance with <1ms p99 latency.

## Key Architecture Features

**Unified API Layer:** The gateway supports calling any LLM (API or self-hosted) through a single unified API with features including streaming, tool use, structured outputs (JSON), batch, embeddings, multimodal (images, files), caching, etc.

**High-Performance Rust Implementation:** The gateway (written in Rust) achieves <1ms P99 latency overhead under extreme load, with LiteLLM adding 25-100x+ more latency than TensorZero's gateway at 10,000 QPS.

**Structured Inference:** The gateway enforces schemas for inputs and outputs, ensuring robustness for your application.

**Multi-Step Workflows:** The gateway provides first-class support for complex multi-step LLM workflows by associating multiple inferences with an episode, allowing feedback to be assigned at the inference or episode level.

## Reliability & Operations

The gateway ensures high availability with routing, retries, fallbacks, load balancing, and granular timeouts. The gateway automatically fallbacks failed inferences to different inference providers, or completely different variants, ensuring misconfiguration, provider downtime, and other edge cases don't affect availability.

## Observability & Experimentation

The gateway collects structured inference traces along with associated downstream metrics and natural-language feedback, with everything stored in a ClickHouse database for real-time analytics. The gateway automatically routes traffic between variants to enable A/B tests.

## Operational Focus

TensorZero targets teams with strong DevOps cultures that treat AI infrastructure with the same rigor as traditional backend systems, delivering structured inference patterns with GitOps-based operations.

# TensorZero Configuration & Deployment Guide

## Configuration Overview

TensorZero uses a **TOML-based configuration system** to define functions, variants, and model routing. The documentation references a "Configuration Reference" for detailed parameters, though specific TOML examples aren't shown in the provided content.

## Docker Deployment

The platform provides containerized deployment via **`tensorzero/gateway`** Docker image. The documentation points to a "Deployment Guide" with detailed instructions, and references indicate support for `docker compose` orchestration patterns suitable for both prototypes and large-scale deployments.

## Credentials Management

TensorZero handles provider API keys through:
- **Environment variable configuration** for secure credential storage
- **Auth system setup** allowing clients to access models without exposing provider API keys directly
- Support for multiple provider credentials (OpenAI, Anthropic, AWS Bedrock, etc.)

## Client API Calls

Three primary integration methods are available:

**1. TensorZero Python SDK:**
```python
from tensorzero import TensorZeroGateway
with TensorZeroGateway.build_embedded(...) as t0:
    response = t0.inference(
        model_name="openai::gpt-4o-mini",
        input={"messages": [{"role": "user", "content": "..."}]}
    )
```

**2. OpenAI SDK Compatibility:**
Patch standard OpenAI clients to route through TensorZero using model strings like `"tensorzero::model_name::openai::gpt-4o-mini"`

**3. HTTP REST API:**
Direct POST requests to `/inference` endpoints with JSON payloads

## OpenAI-Compatible Endpoint

The gateway exposes an **OpenAI v1-compatible API** at `/openai/v1`, enabling drop-in integration with existing OpenAI SDK implementations across Python, JavaScript/TypeScript, and other languages.

The documentation emphasizes a **5-minute Quick Start** demonstrating progression from basic API wrapper to production-ready applications with observability and fine-tuning capabilities.

Based on the search results, here's information about TensorZero configuration with TOML examples for functions, variants, models, credentials, and docker-compose:

## Configuration Structure

The configuration file is a TOML file with major sections including gateway, clickhouse, postgres, models, model_providers, functions, variants, tools, metrics, rate_limiting, and object_storage.

## Function and Variant Configuration Example

A minimal configuration file defines a model and a simple chat function with sections like `[models.gpt_4o_mini_2024_07_18]`, `[models.gpt_4o_mini_2024_07_18.providers.azure]`, and `[functions.my_function_name]` with `[functions.my_function_name.variants.my_variant_name]` specifying the variant type and model.

For example, with Azure:
```toml
[models.gpt_4o_mini_2024_07_18]
routing = ["azure"]

[models.gpt_4o_mini_2024_07_18.providers.azure]
type = "azure"
deployment_id = "gpt4o-mini-20240718"
endpoint = "https://your-azure-openai-endpoint.openai.azure.com"

[functions.my_function_name]
type = "chat"

[functions.my_function_name.variants.my_variant_name]
type = "chat_completion"
model = "gpt_4o_mini_2024_07_18"
```

## Credential Management

You can customize the credential location by setting the api_key_location to env::YOUR_ENVIRONMENT_VARIABLE or dynamic::ARGUMENT_NAME.

## Docker Compose Setup

The Docker Compose configuration sets up a development ClickHouse database (where TensorZero stores data), the TensorZero Gateway, and the TensorZero UI. The gateway service mounts the tensorzero.toml file into the container and sets environment variables for ClickHouse URL and API keys.

# TensorZero Gateway Configuration Reference Summary

## Core Configuration Structure

The configuration uses TOML format with major sections: `gateway`, `clickhouse`, `postgres`, `models`, `model_providers`, `functions`, `variants`, `tools`, `metrics`, `rate_limiting`, and `object_storage`.

## Gateway Section (`[gateway]`)

**Binding & Network:**
- `bind_address`: Socket address (default: `[::]:3000`)
- `base_path`: HTTP endpoint prefix (default: `/`)

**Authentication:**
- `auth.enabled`: Enable API key authentication (default: `false`)
- `auth.cache.enabled`: Cache auth database queries (default: `true`)
- `auth.cache.ttl_ms`: Cache lifetime in milliseconds (default: `1000`)

**Observability:**
- `observability.enabled`: Enable ClickHouse integration (default: `null`)
- `observability.async_writes`: Offload writes to background tasks
- `observability.batch_writes`: Batch multiple records for efficiency
- `observability.disable_automatic_migrations`: Skip auto-migrations

**Performance:**
- `cache.valkey.ttl_s`: Inference cache TTL (default: `86400` seconds)
- `global_outbound_http_timeout_ms`: HTTP request timeout (default: `900000`)
- `debug`: Enable verbose logging (default: `false`)

**Advanced:**
- `relay.gateway_url`: Forward requests through another gateway
- `relay.api_key_location`: Relay gateway authentication
- `export.otlp.traces.enabled`: OpenTelemetry trace export
- `template_filesystem_access.base_path`: Enable template includes

## Models Configuration (`[models.model_name]`)

**Required Fields:**
- `routing`: Array of provider names for request forwarding

**Optional Fields:**
- `skip_relay`: Bypass relay gateway (default: `false`)
- `timeouts.non_streaming.total_ms`: Non-streaming timeout
- `timeouts.streaming.ttft_ms`: Time-to-first-token timeout
- `timeouts.streaming.total_ms`: Total streaming timeout

## Provider Configuration (`[models.model_name.providers.provider_name]`)

**Required:**
- `type`: Provider type (anthropic, openai, azure, aws_bedrock, etc.)

**Common Fields:**
- `api_key_location`: Credential source (`env::VAR`, `dynamic::ARG`, `none`)
- `timeouts`: Request-specific timeouts
- `extra_body`: Modify request body via JSON Pointer
- `extra_headers`: Override HTTP headers

**Provider-Specific Examples:**

| Provider | Key Fields |
|----------|-----------|
| **anthropic** | `model_name`, `api_key_location`, `api_base` |
| **openai** | `model_name`, `api_base`, `api_key_location` |
| **azure** | `deployment_id`, `endpoint`, `api_key_location` |
| **aws_bedrock** | `model_id`, `region`, `access_key_id`, `secret_access_key` |
| **vllm** | `api_base`, `model_name`, `api_key_location` |

## Credential Management

Credentials support multiple location types:
- `env::VARIABLE_NAME` - Environment variable at startup
- `dynamic::argument_name` - Resolved at request time
- `path::/path/to/file` - File-based credentials
- `sdk` - Provider SDK auto-discovery (AWS services)
- `none` - No authentication

Fallback support via objects:
```toml
api_key_location = { default = "dynamic::key", fallback = "env::BACKUP_KEY" }
```

## Functions (`[functions.function_name]`)

**Required:**
- `type`: Either `"chat"` or `"json"`

**Schema Definitions:**
- `system_schema`: JSON Schema for system messages
- `user_schema`: JSON Schema for user messages
- `assistant_schema`: JSON Schema for assistant responses
- `output_schema` (json only): Schema for structured output

**Chat-Specific:**
- `tools`: Array of tool names available
- `tool_choice`: `"none"`, `"auto"`, `"required"`, or `{ specific = "tool_name" }`
- `parallel_tool_calls`: Allow multiple tools per turn

## Variants (`[functions.function_name.variants.variant_name]`)

- `type`: Variant implementation type
- Template paths for rendering messages
- Model and provider assignment
- Custom timeouts and configurations

## Embedding Models (`[embedding_models.model_name]`)

- `routing`: Provider routing list
- `timeout_ms`: Overall model timeout
- Provider configuration mirrors standard models

## Provider Type Defaults (`[provider_types.provider_name]`)

Set global defaults for credential locations:
```toml
[provider_types.openai.defaults]
api_key_location = "dynamic::openai_api_key"
```

Supported: anthropic, azure, deepseek, fireworks, gcp_vertex_*, google_ai_studio, groq, mistral, openai, openrouter, together, xai.

## Key Configuration Patterns

**Dynamic Routing with Fallback:**
```toml
[models.gpt-4o]
routing = ["primary_provider", "fallback_provider"]
```

**Timeout Hierarchy:**
Global → Model → Provider → Variant (most specific wins)

**Credential Fallback:**
Primary source attempted first; fallback activated on failure

**Request Customization:**
Use `extra_body` and `extra_headers` for provider-specific parameters without explicit TensorZero support
```

# TensorZero Quickstart Guide Summary

## Docker Compose Setup

The provided configuration deploys three services:

- **ClickHouse**: Database storing inference data on port 8123
- **Gateway**: API server on port 3000 handling inference requests
- **UI**: Dashboard on port 4000 for observability and fine-tuning

Key environment variables required:
- `OPENAI_API_KEY` for model access
- `TENSORZERO_CLICKHOUSE_URL` for database connectivity

## TOML Configuration

The minimal setup uses a single configuration file defining functions and variants:

```toml
[functions.generate_haiku]
type = "chat"

[functions.generate_haiku.variants.gpt_4o_mini]
type = "chat_completion"
model = "openai::gpt-4o-mini"
```

This declares a function with one implementation using GPT-4o mini via the shorthand notation.

## Calling the Gateway

**HTTP API approach:**
```
POST http://localhost:3000/inference
```
Supply function_name and input parameters as JSON payload.

**Python client:**
Install `tensorzero` package and use embedded or service-based initialization with ClickHouse connectivity.

**OpenAI-compatible interface:**
The gateway emulates OpenAI's API structure at `http://localhost:3000/openai/v1`, accepting standard ChatCompletion requests with model identifier `tensorzero::function_name::generate_haiku`.

## Multi-Provider Handling

While the quickstart demonstrates basic OpenAI integration, the documentation indicates production deployments support "multiple providers, routing, and fallbacks" through expanded configuration options not detailed in this guide.

Credential management occurs through environment variables, allowing provider-specific API keys to be injected at runtime.

## OpenAI-Compatible Endpoint Configuration for Claude Agent SDK

For cases where the LLM provider has an OpenAI compatible API endpoint, you can set the base_url and api_key. The OpenAI Agents SDK documentation provides guidance on this configuration approach.

A configurable example is available in examples/model_providers/custom_example_global.py, which demonstrates how to set up custom OpenAI-compatible providers globally.

### Configuration Methods

The SDK supports multiple ways to configure custom providers:

1. **Global OpenAI Client**: You can use set_default_openai_client for cases where you want to globally use an instance of AsyncOpenAI as the LLM client

2. **Runner Level**: ModelProvider is at the Runner.run level, which lets you say "use a custom model provider for all agents in this run", with a configurable example in examples/model_providers/custom_example_provider.py

3. **Agent Level**: Agent.model lets you specify the model on a specific Agent instance, enabling you to mix and match different providers for different agents

However, the search results don't contain specific information about TensorZero's OpenAI-compatible endpoint configuration or how it integrates specifically with the Claude Agent SDK's `base_url` parameter. You may want to check the TensorZero documentation directly or review the examples mentioned in the OpenAI Agents SDK repository for more detailed implementation guidance.

Based on the search results, here's comprehensive information about configuring Claude Agent SDK with custom LLM providers using `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` and proxy configurations:

## Key Configuration Variables

The Claude Agent SDK can be pointed to a custom proxy by setting `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` environment variable to a proxy URL like `http://localhost:4000`.

## Proxy Setup with LiteLLM

The Claude Agent SDK can use any LLM provider through LiteLLM Proxy, allowing you to use the same agent code with OpenAI, Bedrock, Azure, Vertex AI, or any other provider.

To configure the Claude Agent SDK with LiteLLM, set environment variables:
```
ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=http://localhost:4000
ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=sk-1234 # Your LiteLLM key
```

## Gateway Requirements

For an LLM gateway to work with Claude Code, it must expose either the Anthropic Messages format (`/v1/messages`, `/v1/messages/count_tokens`), forward request headers (`anthropic-beta`, `anthropic-version`), or support Bedrock/Vertex alternatives.

## Additional Configuration Options

You can also configure `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=http://localhost:4000/v1` to use LiteLLM's Anthropic/OpenAI-compatible endpoint.

For custom headers, use `ANTHROPIC_CUSTOM_HEADERS` to pass additional authentication or configuration data to your gateway.

If you have configured custom model names in your gateway, use environment variables documented in Model configuration to match your custom names.

# Claude Agent SDK Connection to Proxy

Based on the documentation provided, here's how the Claude Agent SDK connects to a proxy:

## Connection Method

The SDK connects via environment variables that override the default Anthropic endpoint:

- **`ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`**: Points to the proxy location (e.g., `http://localhost:4000`)
- **`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`**: Authenticates with the proxy using a proxy-specific key (not an Anthropic key)

## API Format Expected

The documentation indicates the SDK expects "the same agent code" to work across providers when pointed at LiteLLM. The configuration shown uses the standard Anthropic SDK client (`ClaudeSDKClient`) with a model parameter that references proxy-configured models.

## Key Implementation Detail

> "Point to LiteLLM proxy (not Anthropic)"

The SDK appears to use the **Anthropic Messages API format** since it's the official Claude Agent SDK, but the critical requirement for TensorZero is supporting the `/v1/messages` endpoint structure that accepts the same request/response format the Claude SDK natively expects.

## Authentication

Authentication uses a simple API key header mechanism compatible with the LiteLLM proxy's standard key format (`sk-*`), not requiring Anthropic's direct authentication.

**For TensorZero**: You need to expose an endpoint compatible with Anthropic's Messages API format, accepting the standard authentication header pattern LiteLLM uses.

## TensorZero and Anthropic Integration

TensorZero supports the Anthropic messages API through an inference endpoint at `http://localhost:3000/inference`. The integration allows you to send requests with Anthropic model names and message structures.

If you're using the OpenAI-Compatible Inference API, TensorZero uses `tensorzero::extra_body` instead of the standard `extra_body` parameter.

## Configuration

To set up TensorZero with Anthropic models, you need to configure a model and provider in your configuration file, specifying the model name (e.g., "claude-haiku-4-5") with the anthropic provider type.

TensorZero also supports Azure-hosted Anthropic models by configuring the anthropic provider type with an api_base pointing to your Azure resource endpoint.

The search results show that TensorZero provides integration with Anthropic's message API, but they don't specifically mention a native `/v1/messages` compatible endpoint that mimics Anthropic's own API structure. The primary interaction appears to be through TensorZero's own `/inference` endpoint rather than a direct `/v1/messages` proxy endpoint.

# Anthropic Provider Configuration for TensorZero

## TOML Configuration Format

The basic configuration structure:

```toml
[models.claude_haiku_4_5]
routing = ["anthropic"]

[models.claude_haiku_4_5.providers.anthropic]
type = "anthropic"
model_name = "claude-haiku-4-5"
```

## Model Name Format

Two approaches exist for specifying models:

1. **Short-hand syntax**: Use `"anthropic::model_name"` directly in variants without detailed configuration
2. **Full configuration**: Define models with explicit provider settings in the config file

## API Key Configuration

"You must set the `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` environment variable before running the gateway." Alternative credential handling supports `env::YOUR_ENVIRONMENT_VARIABLE` or `dynamic::ARGUMENT_NAME` patterns via the Credential Management guide.

## Alternative Endpoints

TensorZero supports deploying Anthropic models through third-party platforms:
- **AWS Bedrock**: Uses `aws_bedrock` provider type with model IDs and region specification
- **Azure AI Foundry**: Overrides the API base URL while maintaining the anthropic provider type
- **GCP Vertex AI**: Uses `gcp_vertex_anthropic` provider with project ID and location settings

## API Proxy Capability

The documentation does not explicitly indicate whether TensorZero exposes an `/v1/messages` endpoint to act as a direct Anthropic API proxy.

## Streaming Support

No streaming implementation details appear in the provided content.

Based on the search results, here's information about using TensorZero with Claude Code and the `/openai/v1` endpoint:

## TensorZero Integration

The TensorZero Gateway runs on http://localhost:3000 by default, and you can override this by specifying the base_url in your configuration.

## Using with Claude Code via ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL

Claude Code can work with proxy services that provide `/v1/messages` endpoint support, and the proxy is designed to work seamlessly with Claude Code CLI by setting ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL to point to the proxy endpoint.

For TensorZero specifically, you can configure it as a proxy pass-through for Claude Code by:

1. **Setting up TensorZero** with your model providers (like Anthropic) in the configuration
2. **Routing Claude Code requests** through TensorZero by setting the environment variable:
   ```
   ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=http://localhost:3000
   ```

## OpenAI-Compatible API Support

If you're using the OpenAI-Compatible Inference API with TensorZero, use tensorzero::extra_body instead. This means TensorZero can accept OpenAI-compatible requests and proxy them appropriately.

Any OpenAI-compatible API can be used by setting the appropriate OPENAI_BASE_URL. This applies to TensorZero as well when configured with OpenAI-compatible endpoints.

The key is that TensorZero acts as a gateway/proxy that can accept requests from Claude Code via `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` environment variable configuration and route them to your configured model providers (including Anthropic, Azure, or AWS Bedrock).


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.

Based on the web search results, here's what I found about TensorZero's Anthropic integration:

## TensorZero Anthropic Integration

TensorZero provides guidance on setting up a minimal deployment to use the TensorZero Gateway with the Anthropic API. You can use the short-hand `anthropic::model_name` to use an Anthropic model with TensorZero, and you can use Anthropic models in TensorZero variants by setting the model field to `anthropic::model_name`.

TensorZero supports making inference requests to the `/inference` endpoint with Anthropic models like `anthropic::claude-haiku-4-5`, including system prompts, messages, and extra body parameters like cache control.

## v1/messages Endpoint Support

While the search results show that TensorZero can use Anthropic models on Azure AI Foundry by overriding the API base to point to Azure's Anthropic endpoint at `https://YOUR-RESOURCE-NAME.services.ai.azure.com/anthropic/v1/`, the results don't contain specific information about a direct passthrough proxy mode for the `/v1/messages` endpoint.

However, I did find that LiteLLM offers pass-through endpoints for Anthropic that call the provider-specific endpoint in native format with no translation, allowing you to replace `https://api.anthropic.com` with the LiteLLM proxy base URL at `/anthropic/v1/messages`, which suggests this is a pattern used in the ecosystem.

The search results focus primarily on TensorZero's inference API and OpenAI-compatible endpoints rather than detailing a specific `/v1/messages` passthrough mode for 2025-2026.

> AGENT

Now let me propose approaches. Based on your answers, here's a summary of what we're designing:

- **Multi-provider LLM support** via TensorZero (OpenAI, Anthropic, Google, Mistral, etc.)
- **TensorZero replaces proxy.py** as the credential proxy
- **Adapter in backend** translates Anthropic API format to TensorZero format
- **Gateway only** deployment (no ClickHouse/UI initially)

## Approach 1: Anthropic-to-TensorZero Adapter (Recommended)

```
Sidecar (Claude Agent SDK)
  → ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL → Backend /anthropic/v1/messages
  → Backend adapter translates request → TensorZero /inference
  → TensorZero routes to configured provider (Anthropic, OpenAI, Google, etc.)
  → Response translated back to Anthropic format → Sidecar
```

**Trade-offs:**
- (+) Sidecar code unchanged - still uses Claude Agent SDK natively
- (+) Single adapter handles all format translation
- (+) TensorZero manages all API keys centrally
- (+) Model selection via config or per-request parameter
- (-) Must handle streaming translation (Anthropic SSE ↔ TensorZero SSE)
- (-) Feature gaps between providers (e.g., extended thinking is Anthropic-only)

## Approach 2: OpenAI SDK in Sidecar + TensorZero

```
Sidecar (switched to OpenAI SDK)
  → TensorZero /openai/v1/chat/completions
  → TensorZero routes to provider
```

**Trade-offs:**
- (+) TensorZero's OpenAI-compatible endpoint works natively
- (+) No translation layer needed
- (-) Loses Claude Agent SDK features (subagents, MCP, tool orchestration)
- (-) Major sidecar rewrite required
- (-) OpenAI format lacks Anthropic-specific features

## Approach 3: Direct Provider Proxy with TensorZero Config

```
Backend adapter reads TensorZero config
  → Routes directly to providers using native APIs
  → Skip TensorZero gateway entirely, use just its config format
```

**Trade-offs:**
- (+) No extra container (TensorZero gateway)
- (-) Must implement routing/fallback logic ourselves
- (-) Loses TensorZero's Rust performance and reliability features
- (-) More code to maintain

**I recommend Approach 1.** It preserves the Claude Agent SDK's powerful features (subagents, MCP tools) while adding multi-provider support through TensorZero. The adapter is a bounded piece of work in the existing backend.

     1→import logging
     2→import uuid
     3→from datetime import datetime, timedelta, timezone
     4→
     5→from fastapi import APIRouter, HTTPException, Request
     6→from fastapi.responses import StreamingResponse
     7→import httpx
     8→
     9→from app.config import ANTHROPIC_API_KEY
    10→
    11→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    12→
    13→ANTHROPIC_UPSTREAM = "https://api.anthropic.com"
    14→
    15→_SKIP_REQUEST_HEADERS = {
    16→    "host", "content-length", "transfer-encoding", "connection",
    17→    "x-api-key",         # client must not override billing identity
    18→    "anthropic-version", # proxy controls API version
    19→    # anthropic-beta is intentionally NOT blocked: Claude Code sends beta body
    20→    # fields (e.g. context_management) alongside the matching beta header, and
    21→    # Anthropic rejects those fields as "extra inputs" if the header is absent.
    22→}
    23→_SKIP_RESPONSE_HEADERS = {"transfer-encoding", "content-encoding", "connection"}
    24→
    25→
    26→class ProxyTokenStore:
    27→    def __init__(self, ttl_seconds: int = 600):
    28→        self._ttl = ttl_seconds
    29→        self._tokens: dict[str, datetime] = {}
    30→
    31→    def create_token(self) -> str:
    32→        token = str(uuid.uuid4())
    33→        self._tokens[token] = datetime.now(timezone.utc) + timedelta(seconds=self._ttl)
    34→        return token
    35→
    36→    def validate_token(self, token: str) -> bool:
    37→        # Tokens are intentionally multi-use: Claude Code makes many API calls
    38→        # per session, all protected by the same UUID. The token is valid for
    39→        # the session TTL (600s safety net) but is explicitly revoked by
    40→        # proxy_token_store.revoke_token() in agent.py's finally block when
    41→        # the Claude Code subprocess exits. Do not make single-use.
    42→        expiry = self._tokens.get(token)
    43→        if expiry is None:
    44→            return False
    45→        if datetime.now(timezone.utc) > expiry:
    46→            self._tokens.pop(token, None)
    47→            return False
    48→        return True
    49→
    50→    def revoke_token(self, token: str) -> None:
    51→        self._tokens.pop(token, None)
    52→
    53→    def cleanup_expired(self) -> int:
    54→        now = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
    55→        expired = [t for t, exp in self._tokens.items() if now > exp]
    56→        for t in expired:
    57→            del self._tokens[t]
    58→        return len(expired)
    59→
    60→
    61→proxy_token_store = ProxyTokenStore()
    62→
    63→router = APIRouter(prefix="/anthropic")
    64→
    65→
    66→# Read-only discovery endpoints that the Claude Code SDK calls during startup
    67→# (e.g. GET /v1/models to resolve short model aliases like "sonnet").
    68→# These carry no session-specific data, so we forward them with the real API
    69→# key without requiring a registered session token.
    70→_UNAUTHENTICATED_PASSTHROUGH = {"v1/models"}
    71→
    72→
    73→@router.api_route("/{path:path}", methods=["GET", "POST", "PUT", "DELETE", "PATCH"])
    74→async def proxy_anthropic(path: str, request: Request):
    75→    # Allow a small set of read-only discovery endpoints to pass through
    76→    # without a session token so the SDK can resolve short model names.
    77→    if path not in _UNAUTHENTICATED_PASSTHROUGH:
    78→        # Claude Code CLI sends the API key as x-api-key (Anthropic SDK default).
    79→        # Fall back to Authorization: Bearer for other clients.
    80→        session_token = (
    81→            request.headers.get("x-api-key")
    82→            or request.headers.get("authorization", "")[len("Bearer "):]
    83→            or None
    84→        )
    85→        if not session_token or not proxy_token_store.validate_token(session_token):
    86→            raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="Invalid or expired session token")
    87→
    88→    headers = {
    89→        k: v for k, v in request.headers.items()
    90→        if k.lower() not in _SKIP_REQUEST_HEADERS
    91→    }
    92→    headers["x-api-key"] = ANTHROPIC_API_KEY
    93→    headers["anthropic-version"] = "2023-06-01"
    94→
    95→    body = await request.body()
    96→
    97→    # Use a fresh client per request: streaming responses that are cut short
    98→    # (e.g. client disconnects) leave connections in a partial read state, and
    99→    # reusing a shared SSL context across those connections causes
   100→    # SSLV3_ALERT_BAD_RECORD_MAC / record layer failure errors.
   101→    # Query params are intentionally NOT forwarded — Claude Code appends
   102→    # internal params (e.g. ?beta=true) that Anthropic rejects as "extra inputs".
   103→    client = httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=httpx.Timeout(300.0))
   104→    try:
   105→        upstream_req = client.build_request(
   106→            method=request.method,
   107→            url=f"{ANTHROPIC_UPSTREAM}/{path}",
   108→            headers=headers,
   109→            content=body,
   110→        )
   111→        upstream_resp = await client.send(upstream_req, stream=True)
   112→
   113→        response_headers = {
   114→            k: v for k, v in upstream_resp.headers.items()
   115→            if k.lower() not in _SKIP_RESPONSE_HEADERS
   116→        }
   117→
   118→        async def body_generator():
   119→            try:
   120→                async for chunk in upstream_resp.aiter_bytes():
   121→                    yield chunk
   122→            finally:
   123→                await upstream_resp.aclose()
   124→                await client.aclose()
   125→
   126→        return StreamingResponse(
   127→            body_generator(),
   128→            status_code=upstream_resp.status_code,
   129→            headers=response_headers,
   130→        )
   131→    except Exception:
   132→        await client.aclose()
   133→        logger.exception("Proxy request to upstream failed: %s %s", request.method, path)
   134→        raise
   135→
```

     1→import express, { Request, Response } from "express";
     2→import { query, AgentDefinition } from "@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk";

     9→// Claude CLI requires certain directories/files under ~/.claude to exist.
    10→// The container uses a tmpfs mount at ~/.claude which starts empty, so we
    11→// create the expected structure at startup.
    12→const claudeDir = join(homedir(), ".claude");
    13→for (const sub of ["debug", "projects"]) {
    14→  const dir = join(claudeDir, sub);
    15→  if (!existsSync(dir)) {
    16→    mkdirSync(dir, { recursive: true });
    17→  }
    18→}

    27→if (process.env.LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY && process.env.LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY) {
    28→  langfuse = new Langfuse();
    29→  console.log("[sidecar] Langfuse tracing enabled");

    32→const app = express();

    36→
    37→// Per-request abort tracking — avoids a global variable whose cleanup in one
    38→// request's `finally` block can clobber a concurrently-started request.
    39→let nextRequestId = 0;
    40→const activeAborts = new Map<number, AbortController>();
    41→
    42→// How long (ms) to wait for the SDK iterator to yield a message before
    43→// aborting.  Resets on every message so active multi-turn queries are not
    44→// interrupted.  Covers cases where the CLI subprocess hangs on startup
    45→// (e.g. broken session resume, unreachable MCP server).
    46→const SDK_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS = 60_000; // 1 minute
    47→
    48→// Timeout (ms) for pre-flight reachability checks against MCP / API URLs.
    49→const PREFLIGHT_TIMEOUT_MS = 10_000; // 10 seconds
    50→
    51→app.get("/health", (_req: Request, res: Response) => {
    52→  const response: HealthResponse = { status: "ok" };
    53→  res.json(response);
    54→});
    55→
    56→app.post("/query", async (req: Request, res: Response) => {
    57→  const body = req.body as QueryRequest;
    58→
    59→  if (!body.message || !body.system_prompt) {
    60→    res.status(400).json({ error: "message and system_prompt are required" });
    61→    return;
    62→  }
    63→
    64→  // Set up SSE headers
    65→  res.setHeader("Content-Type", "text/event-stream");
    66→  res.setHeader("Cache-Control", "no-cache");
    67→  res.setHeader("Connection", "keep-alive");
    68→  res.setHeader("X-Accel-Buffering", "no");
    69→  res.flushHeaders();
    70→
    71→  const abortController = new AbortController();
    72→  const requestId = nextRequestId++;
    73→  activeAborts.set(requestId, abortController);
    74→
    75→  // Merge per-request env overrides (e.g. fresh proxy tokens) with process env
    76→  const sdkEnv: Record<string, string> = {};
    77→  for (const [k, v] of Object.entries(process.env)) {
    78→    if (v !== undefined) sdkEnv[k] = v;
    79→  }
    80→  if (body.env) {

    87→  sdkEnv["LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY"] = "";
    88→
    89→  let responseEnded = false;
    90→
    91→  // Detect client disconnect

    97→
    98→  // --- Langfuse tracing setup ---

   103→  const traceMessage = body.original_message || body.message;
   104→  const traceInput: Record<string, unknown> = {

   106→  };
   107→  if (body.conversation_history && body.conversation_history.length > 0) {
   108→    traceInput.conversation_history = body.conversation_history;
   109→  }

   116→
   117→  // Per-turn usage tracking from stream events
   118→  let currentGenUsage: { input?: number; output?: number } = {};
   119→  // Accumulated messages for generation input context
   120→  const accumulatedMessages: Array<{ role: string; content: unknown }> = [
   121→    { role: "user", content: body.message },

   123→
   124→  // Collect stderr from the CLI subprocess for debugging
   125→  const stderrLines: string[] = [];
   126→
   127→  // Declared here so `finally` can clear it even if `try` throws early
   128→  let idleTimer: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | null = null;
   129→
   130→  try {

   132→    // The CLI subprocess will hang silently if it can't reach the MCP SSE

   138→    );
   139→
   140→    if (body.mcp_server_url) {
   141→      try {
   142→        const mcpResp = await fetch(body.mcp_server_url, {
   143→          signal: AbortSignal.timeout(PREFLIGHT_TIMEOUT_MS),
   144→        });
   145→        // SSE endpoints normally return 200 with text/event-stream; any
   146→        // non-error status is fine — we just need to know the host is up.
   147→        mcpResp.body?.cancel(); // don't consume the stream
   148→        console.log(`[sidecar] MCP reachability OK (status=${mcpResp.status})`);
   149→      } catch (e: unknown) {
   150→        const reason = e instanceof Error ? e.message : String(e);
   151→        throw new Error(

   154→        );
   155→      }
   156→    }
   157→
   158→    if (apiBase) {

   167→      } catch (e: unknown) {

   169→        throw new Error(

   171→          `Check that PROXY_BASE_URL is reachable from inside the container.`
   172→        );
   173→      }
   174→    }
   175→
   176→    // Convert agents payload from the backend into SDK AgentDefinition objects
   177→    let sdkAgents: Record<string, AgentDefinition> | undefined;
   178→    if (body.agents) {

   183→          prompt: def.prompt,
   184→          ...(def.tools ? { tools: def.tools } : {}),
   185→          ...(def.model ? { model: def.model as AgentDefinition["model"] } : {}),
   186→        };
   187→      }
   188→    }
   189→
   190→    const sdkQuery = query({
   191→      prompt: body.message,
   192→      options: {
   193→        model: modelName,
   194→        systemPrompt: body.system_prompt,
   195→        allowedTools: ["Task", "mcp__duckdb__execute_sql"],
   196→        permissionMode: "bypassPermissions",
   197→        allowDangerouslySkipPermissions: true,
   198→        maxTurns: 20,
   199→        includePartialMessages: true,
   200→        abortController,
   201→        env: sdkEnv,
   202→        stderr: (line: string) => {
   203→          stderrLines.push(line);
   204→          console.error(`[sidecar:cli] ${line}`);
   205→        },
   206→        ...(sdkAgents ? { agents: sdkAgents } : {}),
   207→        ...(body.mcp_server_url

   209→              mcpServers: {
   210→                duckdb: {
   211→                  type: "sse" as const,
   212→                  url: body.mcp_server_url,
   213→                },
   214→              },
   215→            }
   216→          : {}),
   217→        ...(body.session_id ? { resume: body.session_id } : {}),

   220→
   221→    console.log(
   222→      `[sidecar] SDK query started model=${modelName} reqId=${requestId}`
   223→    );
   224→
   225→    // Idle-timeout: abort if no message arrives within SDK_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS.
   226→    // The timer resets on every message so long-running multi-turn queries
   227→    // that are making progress are not interrupted.
   228→    idleTimer = setTimeout(() => {
   229→      console.error(`[sidecar] SDK idle timeout (${SDK_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS}ms) reached, aborting reqId=${requestId}`);
   230→      abortController.abort();
   231→    }, SDK_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS);
   232→
   233→    for await (const message of sdkQuery) {
   234→      // Reset idle timer on every message
   235→      if (idleTimer) { clearTimeout(idleTimer); }
   236→      idleTimer = setTimeout(() => {
   237→        console.error(`[sidecar] SDK idle timeout (${SDK_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS}ms) reached, aborting reqId=${requestId}`);
   238→        abortController.abort();
   239→      }, SDK_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS);
   240→
   241→      if (responseEnded) break;
   242→      // Forward each SDK message as an SSE data line
   243→      res.write(`data: ${JSON.stringify(message)}\n\n`);
   244→
   245→      // --- Create Langfuse observations from SDK messages ---
   246→      if (!trace) continue;
   247→      const msg = message as Record<string, unknown>;
   248→
   249→      if (msg.type === "stream_event") {
   250→        // Capture per-turn token usage from API stream events
   251→        const event = (msg.event as Record<string, unknown>) || {};
   252→        const eventType = event.type as string;
   253→        if (eventType === "message_start") {
   254→          const msgData = (event.message as Record<string, unknown>) || {};
   255→          const usage = (msgData.usage as Record<string, number>) || {};
   256→          currentGenUsage = { input: usage.input_tokens || 0 };
   257→        } else if (eventType === "message_delta") {
   258→          const usage = (event.usage as Record<string, number>) || {};
   259→          currentGenUsage.output = usage.output_tokens || 0;
   260→        }
   261→      } else if (msg.type === "assistant") {
   262→        // Create a generation observation for each assistant turn
   263→        const msgObj = (msg.message as Record<string, unknown>) || {};
   264→        const content = msgObj.content as unknown[];
   265→
   266→        const usage =
   267→          currentGenUsage.input !== undefined
   268→            ? {
   269→                input: currentGenUsage.input || 0,
   270→                output: currentGenUsage.output || 0,
   271→                total:
   272→                  (currentGenUsage.input || 0) +
   273→                  (currentGenUsage.output || 0),
   274→                unit: "TOKENS" as const,
   275→              }
   276→            : undefined;
   277→
   278→        const gen = trace.generation({
   279→          name: "claude.assistant.turn",
   280→          model: (msgObj.model as string) || modelName,
   281→          input: { messages: accumulatedMessages.slice(-6) },
   282→          output: { content, role: "assistant" },
   283→          usage,
   284→        });
   285→        gen.end();
   286→        currentGenUsage = {};
   287→
   288→        // Accumulate for next turn's input context
   289→        accumulatedMessages.push({ role: "assistant", content });
   290→      } else if (msg.type === "user") {
   291→        // Accumulate tool results for next turn's input context
   292→        const msgObj = (msg.message as Record<string, unknown>) || {};
   293→        accumulatedMessages.push({
   294→          role: "user",
   295→          content: msgObj.content,
   296→        });
   297→      } else if (msg.type === "result") {
   298→        // Finalize trace — mirrors backend's finally block:
   299→        //   trace_session_id = langfuse_session_id or actual_session_id
   300→        const actualSessionId = (msg.session_id as string | undefined) || body.session_id;
   301→        const traceSessionId = body.langfuse_session_id || actualSessionId;
   302→        trace.update({
   303→          ...(traceSessionId ? { sessionId: traceSessionId } : {}),
   304→          output: { session_id: actualSessionId },
   305→        });
   306→      } else if (msg.type === "system") {

   308→        // (matches backend's propagate_attributes(session_id=langfuse_session_id or session_id))
   309→        const sessionId = msg.session_id as string | undefined;
   310→        if (sessionId && !body.langfuse_session_id) {
   311→          trace.update({ sessionId });
   312→        }
   313→      }
   314→    }
   315→  } catch (err: unknown) {

   318→    if (stderrLines.length > 0) {
   319→      errMsg += ` | CLI stderr: ${stderrLines.slice(-5).join(" ")}`;
   320→    }

   326→          `data: ${JSON.stringify({ type: "error", message: errMsg })}\n\n`
   327→        );
   328→      }
   329→      if (trace) {
   330→        trace.update({ output: { error: errMsg } });
   331→      }
   332→    }
   333→  } finally {
   334→    if (idleTimer) { clearTimeout(idleTimer); }
   335→    activeAborts.delete(requestId);
   336→    responseEnded = true;
   337→    res.end();
   338→    // Flush Langfuse events before the response is fully closed
   339→    if (langfuse) {
   340→      await langfuse.flushAsync().catch(() => {});
   341→    }
   342→    console.log(`[sidecar] SSE stream ended reqId=${requestId}`);
   343→  }
   344→});
   345→
   346→app.post("/stop", (_req: Request, res: Response) => {
   347→  if (activeAborts.size > 0) {
   348→    for (const [id, controller] of activeAborts) {
   349→      controller.abort();
   350→      activeAborts.delete(id);
   351→    }
   352→    res.json({ status: "stopped" });
   353→  } else {
   354→    res.json({ status: "no_active_session" });
   355→  }
   356→});
   357→
   358→app.listen(PORT, "0.0.0.0", () => {
   359→  console.log(`Sidecar agent server listening on port ${PORT}`);
   360→});
   361→

## Design Section 1: Architecture Overview

Here's the high-level architecture with TensorZero replacing `proxy.py`:

```
Sidecar (Claude Agent SDK)
  │
  │ ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL = http://backend:10000/llm-proxy
  │ ANTHROPIC_API_KEY  = <session-uuid>
  │
  ├─ POST /llm-proxy/v1/messages        (Anthropic Messages API)
  │
  ▼
Backend (FastAPI) — LLM Proxy Adapter
  │
  │ 1. Validate session UUID token (same as today)
  │ 2. Translate Anthropic request → TensorZero /inference
  │ 3. Stream TensorZero response → Anthropic SSE format
  │
  ├─ POST http://tensorzero:3000/inference
  │
  ▼
TensorZero Gateway (Docker container)
  │
  │ Reads tensorzero.toml for model/provider config
  │ Holds all provider API keys (ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, OPENAI_API_KEY, etc.)
  │
  ├─ Routes to configured provider
  │
  ▼
LLM Provider (Anthropic, OpenAI, Google, Mistral, etc.)
```

**Key changes from current architecture:**
- `proxy.py` replaced by a new `llm_proxy.py` module
- `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` moves from backend `.env` to TensorZero's env
- TensorZero added as a new service in `docker-compose.yml`
- New `tensorzero.toml` config file for model/provider definitions
- Model selection configurable per-request or via TensorZero routing

## Design Section 2: Anthropic-to-TensorZero Translation Layer

The adapter (`llm_proxy.py`) must translate between two API formats:

**Anthropic Messages API (incoming from SDK):**
```json
POST /v1/messages
{
  "model": "claude-sonnet-4-6",
  "max_tokens": 8192,
  "messages": [{"role": "user", "content": "..."}],
  "system": "...",
  "stream": true,
  "tools": [...],
  "metadata": {...}
}
```

**TensorZero Inference API (outgoing to gateway):**
```json
POST /inference
{
  "function_name": "chat",
  "input": {
    "messages": [{"role": "user", "content": [{"type": "text", "value": "..."}]}]
  },
  "stream": true,
  "params": {
    "chat_completion": {
      "max_tokens": 8192
    }
  }
}
```

**Translation responsibilities:**
1. **Request translation**: Convert Anthropic message format → TensorZero input format
2. **Streaming translation**: Convert TensorZero SSE events → Anthropic SSE events (content_block_delta, message_start, message_delta, etc.)
3. **Model mapping**: Map Anthropic model names → TensorZero function/variant names
4. **Tool forwarding**: Pass tool definitions through to the provider
5. **Error mapping**: Convert TensorZero errors → Anthropic error format

**Model name mapping strategy:**
- The sidecar sends model names like `claude-sonnet-4-6`, `haiku`, etc.
- The adapter maps these to TensorZero function names defined in `tensorzero.toml`
- A model mapping config (env var or config file) handles the translation

**What stays the same:**
- UUID session token mechanism (validation before forwarding)
- `/v1/models` passthrough for SDK discovery
- Streaming response format back to sidecar

## Design Section 3: TensorZero Configuration & Deployment

**`tensorzero.toml`** — defines all models and providers:

```toml
[gateway]
bind_address = "[::]:3000"

# Disable observability initially (gateway-only mode)
[gateway.observability]
enabled = false

# ──── Models ────

[models.claude_sonnet]
routing = ["anthropic"]

[models.claude_sonnet.providers.anthropic]
type = "anthropic"
model_name = "claude-sonnet-4-6"

[models.claude_haiku]
routing = ["anthropic"]

[models.claude_haiku.providers.anthropic]
type = "anthropic"
model_name = "claude-haiku-4-5"

[models.gpt_4o]
routing = ["openai"]

[models.gpt_4o.providers.openai]
type = "openai"
model_name = "gpt-4o"

[models.gemini_pro]
routing = ["google"]

[models.gemini_pro.providers.google]
type = "google_ai_studio"
model_name = "gemini-2.0-flash"

[models.mistral_large]
routing = ["mistral"]

[models.mistral_large.providers.mistral]
type = "mistral"
model_name = "mistral-large-latest"

# ──── Functions ────

[functions.chat]
type = "chat"

[functions.chat.variants.default]
type = "chat_completion"
model = "claude_sonnet"
```

**Docker Compose addition:**

```yaml
services:
  tensorzero:
    image: tensorzero/gateway:latest
    ports: ["3000:3000"]
    volumes:
      - ./tensorzero.toml:/etc/tensorzero/tensorzero.toml:ro
    environment:
      - ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=${ANTHROPIC_API_KEY}
      - OPENAI_API_KEY=${OPENAI_API_KEY}
      - GOOGLE_API_KEY=${GOOGLE_API_KEY}
      - MISTRAL_API_KEY=${MISTRAL_API_KEY}
    networks:
      - agent-sandbox
```

**Environment variable changes:**
- Provider API keys move to TensorZero's environment (not backend)
- Backend gets new `TENSORZERO_BASE_URL` (default: `http://tensorzero:3000` in Docker, `http://localhost:3000` in dev)
- `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` in backend becomes optional (only needed if TensorZero is disabled)
- New `LLM_PROVIDER_MODEL` env var to select which TensorZero model to use (default: `claude_sonnet`)

> AGENT

## Design Section 4: Error Handling & Provider Feature Gaps

**Provider-specific feature gaps:**

The Claude Agent SDK uses Anthropic-specific features that not all providers support:
- **Extended thinking** — Anthropic only
- **Tool use with specific schemas** — varies by provider (TensorZero handles translation)
- **System prompts** — universal but format differs
- **Streaming event types** — differ between providers

**Error handling strategy:**
1. **Unsupported features**: If a request uses Anthropic-specific features (e.g., `thinking` blocks) and the target model doesn't support them, the adapter strips those fields and logs a warning. It does NOT fail the request.
2. **Provider errors**: TensorZero returns provider-specific errors. The adapter maps them to Anthropic error format (400/401/429/500) so the SDK handles them gracefully.
3. **TensorZero down**: If TensorZero is unreachable, return a 502 with clear error message. The sidecar's existing error handling surfaces this to the user.
4. **Streaming interruptions**: Same pattern as current proxy — per-request httpx client, cleanup on disconnect.

**Model fallback:**
- TensorZero handles routing/fallback natively via the `routing` array in config
- Example: `routing = ["anthropic", "aws_bedrock"]` — if Anthropic API is down, falls back to Bedrock

**What this design intentionally does NOT do:**
- Does not translate between provider-specific features (thinking blocks, caching)
- Does not guarantee feature parity across providers — some features only work with Anthropic
- Does not add request-level model switching from the frontend (future enhancement)

## Design Section 5: Testing Strategy

**Testing approach:**

1. **Unit tests for the adapter:**
   - Request translation (Anthropic → TensorZero format)
   - Response translation (TensorZero SSE → Anthropic SSE)
   - Model name mapping
   - Error format mapping
   - UUID token validation (carried over from proxy.py tests)

2. **Integration tests:**
   - Mock TensorZero gateway, verify end-to-end request flow
   - Test streaming translation with multi-chunk responses
   - Test tool use round-trips through the adapter

3. **E2E tests:**
   - Existing Playwright tests should pass unchanged (sidecar behavior is transparent)
   - Add a test scenario that verifies model switching via config

**Migration path:**
- The adapter is a drop-in replacement for `proxy.py`
- No sidecar code changes required
- No frontend changes required
- Switchover: update `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` prefix from `/anthropic` to `/llm-proxy` in `agent.py`

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/docs/plans/2026-02-26-tensorzero-llm-proxy-design.md",
  "content": "# TensorZero LLM Proxy Design\n\n## Summary\n\nReplace the existing `proxy.py` Anthropic-only credential proxy with a TensorZero-backed LLM gateway that supports multiple model providers (Anthropic, OpenAI, Google, Mistral, etc.) while also serving as the credential proxy for sidecar containers.\n\n## Problem\n\nThe current architecture (`proxy.py`) only supports Anthropic API calls. It performs UUID token validation and credential swapping, forwarding requests directly to `api.anthropic.com`. This limits the system to Claude models only.\n\n## Goals\n\n1. Support multiple LLM providers (Anthropic, OpenAI, Google, Mistral, and any OpenAI-compatible API) through a unified gateway\n2. Replace `proxy.py` with TensorZero as the credential proxy — API keys live in TensorZero's environment, not the backend\n3. Keep the sidecar (Claude Agent SDK) unchanged — no modifications to sidecar code\n4. Maintain the existing UUID session token mechanism for container isolation\n\n## Architecture\n\n```\nSidecar (Claude Agent SDK)\n  │\n  │ ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL = http://backend:10000/llm-proxy\n  │ ANTHROPIC_API_KEY  = <session-uuid>\n  │\n  ├─ POST /llm-proxy/v1/messages        (Anthropic Messages API)\n  │\n  ▼\nBackend (FastAPI) — LLM Proxy Adapter (llm_proxy.py)\n  │\n  │ 1. Validate session UUID token (same mechanism as proxy.py)\n  │ 2. Translate Anthropic request → TensorZero /inference format\n  │ 3. Stream TensorZero response → Anthropic SSE format\n  │\n  ├─ POST http://tensorzero:3000/inference\n  │\n  ▼\nTensorZero Gateway (Docker container, Rust)\n  │\n  │ Reads tensorzero.toml for model/provider config\n  │ Holds all provider API keys\n  │ <1ms P99 latency overhead\n  │\n  ├─ Routes to configured provider\n  │\n  ▼\nLLM Provider (Anthropic, OpenAI, Google, Mistral, etc.)\n```\n\n### Key Architecture Decisions\n\n- **Adapter in backend**: The Claude Agent SDK speaks Anthropic Messages API format (`/v1/messages`), but TensorZero exposes its own `/inference` API. A translation layer in the FastAPI backend bridges these formats. This keeps sidecar code unchanged.\n- **TensorZero replaces proxy.py**: API keys are centralized in TensorZero's environment. The backend no longer needs `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`.\n- **Session tokens preserved**: The UUID token validation mechanism from `proxy.py` is carried into `llm_proxy.py`. Sidecar containers still receive a short-lived UUID, never real API keys.\n- **Gateway-only deployment**: No ClickHouse or TensorZero UI initially. Observability can be added later.\n\n## Components\n\n### 1. LLM Proxy Adapter (`backend/app/llm_proxy.py`)\n\nReplaces `proxy.py`. Responsibilities:\n\n- **UUID token validation**: Same `ProxyTokenStore` mechanism — create token before container starts, validate on each request, revoke when session ends.\n- **Request translation**: Convert Anthropic Messages API format to TensorZero `/inference` format.\n- **Response streaming translation**: Convert TensorZero SSE events back to Anthropic SSE format (`message_start`, `content_block_delta`, `message_delta`, `message_stop`).\n- **Model mapping**: Map model names from sidecar requests (e.g., `claude-sonnet-4-6`, `haiku`) to TensorZero function/variant names.\n- **`/v1/models` passthrough**: The SDK discovery endpoint is handled locally or proxied to TensorZero.\n\n#### Request Translation\n\nAnthropic Messages API (incoming):\n```json\n{\n  \"model\": \"claude-sonnet-4-6\",\n  \"max_tokens\": 8192,\n  \"messages\": [{\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": \"Hello\"}],\n  \"system\": \"You are helpful\",\n  \"stream\": true,\n  \"tools\": [...]\n}\n```\n\nTensorZero Inference API (outgoing):\n```json\n{\n  \"function_name\": \"chat\",\n  \"input\": {\n    \"messages\": [\n      {\"role\": \"system\", \"content\": [{\"type\": \"text\", \"value\": \"You are helpful\"}]},\n      {\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": [{\"type\": \"text\", \"value\": \"Hello\"}]}\n    ]\n  },\n  \"stream\": true,\n  \"params\": {\n    \"chat_completion\": {\n      \"max_tokens\": 8192,\n      \"tools\": [...]\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n#### Response Streaming Translation\n\nTensorZero streams responses as SSE events. The adapter translates these back to Anthropic's SSE event format:\n\n- `message_start` — emitted at stream start with model info and usage\n- `content_block_start` — emitted for each content block (text, tool_use)\n- `content_block_delta` — emitted for each text or tool input chunk\n- `content_block_stop` — emitted when a content block ends\n- `message_delta` — emitted with stop reason and final usage\n- `message_stop` — emitted at stream end\n\n#### Model Name Mapping\n\nA mapping dict translates SDK model names to TensorZero function variants:\n\n```python\nMODEL_MAP = {\n    \"claude-sonnet-4-6\": \"claude_sonnet\",\n    \"claude-haiku-4-5\": \"claude_haiku\",\n    \"haiku\": \"claude_haiku\",\n    \"sonnet\": \"claude_sonnet\",\n    \"gpt-4o\": \"gpt_4o\",\n    \"gemini-2.0-flash\": \"gemini_flash\",\n}\n```\n\nThe mapping is configurable via environment variable `LLM_MODEL_MAP` (JSON string) or defaults.\n\n### 2. TensorZero Configuration (`tensorzero.toml`)\n\n```toml\n[gateway]\nbind_address = \"[::]:3000\"\n\n[gateway.observability]\nenabled = false\n\n# ──── Anthropic Models ────\n\n[models.claude_sonnet]\nrouting = [\"anthropic\"]\n\n[models.claude_sonnet.providers.anthropic]\ntype = \"anthropic\"\nmodel_name = \"claude-sonnet-4-6\"\n\n[models.claude_haiku]\nrouting = [\"anthropic\"]\n\n[models.claude_haiku.providers.anthropic]\ntype = \"anthropic\"\nmodel_name = \"claude-haiku-4-5\"\n\n[models.claude_opus]\nrouting = [\"anthropic\"]\n\n[models.claude_opus.providers.anthropic]\ntype = \"anthropic\"\nmodel_name = \"claude-opus-4-6\"\n\n# ──── OpenAI Models ────\n\n[models.gpt_4o]\nrouting = [\"openai\"]\n\n[models.gpt_4o.providers.openai]\ntype = \"openai\"\nmodel_name = \"gpt-4o\"\n\n[models.gpt_4o_mini]\nrouting = [\"openai\"]\n\n[models.gpt_4o_mini.providers.openai]\ntype = \"openai\"\nmodel_name = \"gpt-4o-mini\"\n\n# ──── Google Models ────\n\n[models.gemini_flash]\nrouting = [\"google\"]\n\n[models.gemini_flash.providers.google]\ntype = \"google_ai_studio\"\nmodel_name = \"gemini-2.0-flash\"\n\n[models.gemini_pro]\nrouting = [\"google\"]\n\n[models.gemini_pro.providers.google]\ntype = \"google_ai_studio\"\nmodel_name = \"gemini-2.5-pro\"\n\n# ──── Mistral Models ────\n\n[models.mistral_large]\nrouting = [\"mistral\"]\n\n[models.mistral_large.providers.mistral]\ntype = \"mistral\"\nmodel_name = \"mistral-large-latest\"\n\n# ──── Functions ────\n\n[functions.chat]\ntype = \"chat\"\n\n[functions.chat.variants.default]\ntype = \"chat_completion\"\nmodel = \"claude_sonnet\"\n```\n\n### 3. Docker Compose Changes\n\nAdd TensorZero gateway as a new service:\n\n```yaml\nservices:\n  tensorzero:\n    image: tensorzero/gateway:latest\n    volumes:\n      - ./tensorzero.toml:/etc/tensorzero/tensorzero.toml:ro\n    environment:\n      - ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=${ANTHROPIC_API_KEY}\n      - OPENAI_API_KEY=${OPENAI_API_KEY:-}\n      - GOOGLE_API_KEY=${GOOGLE_API_KEY:-}\n      - MISTRAL_API_KEY=${MISTRAL_API_KEY:-}\n    networks:\n      - agent-sandbox\n    restart: unless-stopped\n```\n\nThe `app` service no longer needs `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`. Instead it gets:\n- `TENSORZERO_BASE_URL=http://tensorzero:3000`\n\n### 4. Configuration Changes (`config.py`)\n\nNew environment variables:\n- `TENSORZERO_BASE_URL` — URL of the TensorZero gateway (default: `http://localhost:3000`)\n- `LLM_MODEL_MAP` — optional JSON string for custom model name mappings\n\nDeprecated:\n- `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` — no longer needed in backend (lives in TensorZero env)\n\nUnchanged:\n- `ANTHROPIC_MODEL`, `SQL_SUBAGENT_MODEL`, `CHART_SUBAGENT_MODEL` — still used to tell the sidecar which model to request\n\n### 5. Agent.py Changes\n\nMinimal changes:\n- Update `PROXY_BASE_URL` references from `/anthropic` prefix to `/llm-proxy` prefix\n- Container env: `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` now points to `{PROXY_BASE_URL}/llm-proxy`\n- Token creation/revocation unchanged\n\n## Error Handling\n\n### Provider Feature Gaps\n\nThe Claude Agent SDK uses Anthropic-specific features that not all providers support:\n- **Extended thinking** (`thinking` blocks) — Anthropic only\n- **Prompt caching** (`cache_control`) — Anthropic only\n- **Tool use schemas** — varies by provider (TensorZero handles translation)\n\nStrategy: if a request uses Anthropic-specific features and the target model doesn't support them, the adapter strips those fields and logs a warning. It does NOT fail the request.\n\n### Error Mapping\n\nTensorZero errors are mapped to Anthropic error format:\n- TensorZero 400 → Anthropic 400 (invalid request)\n- TensorZero 401 → Anthropic 401 (invalid credentials)\n- TensorZero 429 → Anthropic 429 (rate limited)\n- TensorZero 500+ → Anthropic 500 (internal error)\n\n### TensorZero Unavailable\n\nIf TensorZero is unreachable, the adapter returns HTTP 502 with a clear error message. The sidecar's existing error handling surfaces this to the user.\n\n### Streaming Interruptions\n\nSame pattern as current `proxy.py` — per-request httpx client, cleanup on disconnect.\n\n## Testing Strategy\n\n1. **Unit tests**: Request/response format translation, model mapping, token validation\n2. **Integration tests**: Mock TensorZero gateway, verify end-to-end streaming\n3. **E2E tests**: Existing Playwright tests should pass unchanged (sidecar behavior is transparent)\n\n## Migration Path\n\n1. Add TensorZero gateway to `docker-compose.yml`\n2. Create `tensorzero.toml` with initial model configs\n3. Implement `llm_proxy.py` adapter\n4. Update `agent.py` to use new `/llm-proxy` prefix\n5. Move API keys from backend `.env` to TensorZero env\n6. Remove old `proxy.py`\n7. Update `.env.example` with new variables\n\nNo sidecar changes. No frontend changes.\n\n## References\n\n- [TensorZero GitHub](https://github.com/tensorzero/tensorzero)\n- [TensorZero Configuration Reference](https://www.tensorzero.com/docs/gateway/configuration-reference)\n- [TensorZero Anthropic Provider](https://www.tensorzero.com/docs/integrations/model-providers/anthropic)\n- [Claude Agent SDK with LiteLLM](https://docs.litellm.ai/docs/tutorials/claude_agent_sdk) (similar proxy pattern)\n"
}
```
